+# Documentaci├│n de Comandos del Proyecto LILY-GO
+
+Este documento detalla todos los comandos disponibles en el sistema, su sintaxis y ejemplos de uso. Los comandos se env├¡an a trav├®s del puerto serial.
+
+## Convenciones
+- Los comandos comienzan generalmente con el prefijo `DVL+`.
+- Los par├ímetros se separan por comas u otros delimitadores seg├║n el comando.
+- La mayor├¡a de las configuraciones persisten en la memoria flash (Preferences) y se mantienen tras un reinicio.
+
+---
+
+## 1. Configuraci├│n del Sistema
+
+### Identificaci├│n y Ubicaci├│n
+
+| Comando | Descripci├│n | Ejemplo |
+| :--- | :--- | :--- |
+| `DVL+ID=<id>` | Establece el identificador del dispositivo. Tambi├®n actualiza el t├│pico MQTT. | `DVL+ID=EQUIPO_01` |
+| `DVL+SLAT=<lat>` | Establece la latitud del dispositivo. | `DVL+SLAT=-34.6037` |
+| `DVL+SLONG=<lon>` | Establece la longitud del dispositivo. | `DVL+SLONG=-58.3816` |
+
+### Trama de Datos y Comunicaci├│n
+
+| Comando | Descripci├│n | Ejemplo |
+| :--- | :--- | :--- |
+| `DVL+SFINI=<marker>` | Establece el marcador de inicio de la trama de datos. | `DVL+SFINI=DATA,` |
+| `DVL+SFFIN=<marker>` | Establece el marcador de fin de la trama de datos. | `DVL+SFFIN=\r` |
+| `DVL+SPARSE=<char>` | Establece el car├ícter separador de campos en la trama. | `DVL+SPARSE=;` |
+| `DVL+STIME=<seg>` | Establece el intervalo de publicaci├│n de datos (en segundos). | `DVL+STIME=60` |
+
+### Habilitaciones (Flags)
+
+| Comando | Descripci├│n | Ejemplo |
+| :--- | :--- | :--- |
+| `DVL+EN_SENSOR` | Habilita la lectura del sensor principal. | `DVL+EN_SENSOR` |
+| `DVL+EN_SERIAL` | Habilita la lectura del puerto serial secundario. (Excluyente con Modbus). | `DVL+EN_SERIAL` |
+| `DVL+EN_MODBUS=<1\|0>` | Habilita (`1`) o deshabilita (`0`) la funcionalidad Modbus. Al habilitar, deshabilita `EN_SERIAL`. | `DVL+EN_MODBUS=1` |
+
+### Actualizaci├│n FOTA
+
+| Comando | Descripci├│n | Ejemplo |
+| :--- | :--- | :--- |
+| `DVL+FOTA=<url>` | Inicia una actualizaci├│n de firmware Over-The-Air desde la URL especificada. | `DVL+FOTA=http://server.com/firmware.bin` |
+
+### Control de Hardware
+
+| Comando | Descripci├│n | Ejemplo |
+| :--- | :--- | :--- |
+| `DVL+RESET` | Reinicia el dispositivo ESP32. | `DVL+RESET` |
+| `DVL+EXP_RESET` | Reinicia la placa expansora (cicla la energ├¡a del pin `SENSOR_POWER_PIN`). | `DVL+EXP_RESET` |
+
+---
+
+## 2. Configuraci├│n ADC (Anal├│gico)
+
+Estos comandos permiten calibrar y configurar el comportamiento de las entradas anal├│gicas (ADC0, ADC1, ADC2).
+
+### Filtro (`SFIL`)
+Configura un filtro de umbral m├¡nimo.
+- **Sintaxis:** `DVL+SFIL=n,m,p`
+  - `n`: ├ìndice del canal (0-2).
+  - `m`: Umbral m├¡nimo.
+  - `p`: Valor de reemplazo si la lectura <= `m`.
+- **Ejemplo:** `DVL+SFIL=0,0.5,0.0` (Si ADC0 <= 0.5, leer├í 0.0).
+
+### Factor de Calibraci├│n (`SFACTOR`)
+Ajusta la lectura con una ecuaci├│n lineal: `Valor = (Lectura * m) + p`.
+- **Sintaxis:** `DVL+SFACTOR=n,m,p`
+  - `n`: ├ìndice del canal (0-2).
+  - `m`: Pendiente (factor multiplicativo).
+  - `p`: Offset.
+- **Ejemplo:** `DVL+SFACTOR=1,1.05,0.2`
+
+### Alisado (`SALI`)
+Configura el n├║mero de muestras para el promedio m├│vil.
+- **Sintaxis:** `DVL+SALI=<val>`
+- **Ejemplo:** `DVL+SALI=50` (Promedio de 50 muestras).
+
+### Tiempo de Muestreo (`STADC`)
+Configura cada cu├ínto se toma una muestra ADC (multiplicador de 100ms).
+- **Sintaxis:** `DVL+STADC=<val>`
+- **Ejemplo:** `DVL+STADC=10` (10 * 100ms = 1 segundo).
+
+---
+
+## 3. Configuraci├│n Modbus
+
+Permite definir tramas Modbus para ser consultadas peri├│dicamente.
+
+### Configurar Trama
+- **Sintaxis:** `DVL+MODBUS=idx,ID,FUNC,ADDR,QTY`
+  - `idx`: ├ìndice de la trama (1-5).
+  - `ID`: ID del esclavo Modbus.
+  - `FUNC`: C├│digo de funci├│n.
+  - `ADDR`: Direcci├│n de memoria inicial.
+  - `QTY`: Cantidad de registros.
+- **Ejemplo:** `DVL+MODBUS=1,1,3,100,2` (Trama 1: Esclavo 1, Funci├│n 3, Direcci├│n 100, 2 Registros).
+
+### Borrar Trama
+- **Sintaxis:** `DVL+MODBUSCLR=<idx|ALL>`
+- **Ejemplo:** 
+  - `DVL+MODBUSCLR=1` (Borra la trama 1).
+  - `DVL+MODBUSCLR=ALL` (Borra todas las tramas).
+
+---
+
+## 4. Comandos de Consulta (Query)
+
+Estos comandos devuelven el estado actual de las configuraciones.
+
+| Comando | Retorna (Ejemplo) | Descripci├│n |
+| :--- | :--- | :--- |
+| `DVL+VER` | `VERSION=v1.0` | Versi├│n del firmware. |
+| `DVL+Qtime` | `TIME=60` | Intervalo de publicaci├│n. |
+| `DVL+QID` | *(Asumido, si existe)* | Consultar ID (no expl├¡cito en c├│digo revisado, ver `DVL+ID` para setear). |
+| `DVL+QLAT` | `LAT=-34.xxx` | ├Ültima latitud configurada. |
+| `DVL+QLONG` | `LONG=-58.xxx` | ├Ültima longitud configurada. |
+| `DVL+QFINI` | `START_MARKER=DATA,` | Marcador de inicio. |
+| `DVL+QFFIN` | `END_MARKER=\r` | Marcador de fin. |
+| `DVL+QPARSE` | `SEPARATOR=,` | Separador de trama. |
+| `DVL+QEN_SENSOR`| `EN_SENSOR=1` | Estado de lectura de sensor. |
+| `DVL+QEN_SERIAL`| `EN_SERIAL=0` | Estado de puerto serial secundario. |
+| `DVL+QEN_MODBUS`| `EN_MODBUS=1` | Estado de Modbus. |
+| `DVL+QMODBUS` | `[OK] Ver detalle por Serial` | Imprime configuraci├│n Modbus detallada. |
+| `DVL+QFIL` | `FIL0=...` | Configuraci├│n de filtros ADC. |
+| `DVL+QFACTOR` | `FAC0=...` | Configuraci├│n de factores ADC. |
+| `DVL+QTADC` | `TIME ADC=10` | Tiempo de muestreo ADC. |
+| `DVL+QALI` | `ALISADO=50` | Valor de alisado ADC. |
+
+---
+
+## 5. Comandos de Expansora (`EXP+`)
+
+Cualquier comando que comience con `EXP+` ser├í retransmitido tal cual al puerto serial secundario (`SensorSerial`). El sistema espera una respuesta de la expansora por hasta 5 segundos.
+
+- **Sintaxis:** `EXP+<cualquier_comando>`
+- **Ejemplo:** `EXP+LEER` (Env├¡a "EXP+LEER" a la expansora y devuelve su respuesta).

+# Descripci├│n del Proyecto LILY-GO
+
+## 1. Visi├│n General
+Este proyecto implementa un firmware para dispositivos **LILYGO T-SIM7000G (ESP32)**, dise├▒ado para aplicaciones de telemetr├¡a IoT. El sistema es capaz de recolectar datos de sensores, obtener ubicaci├│n GPS y transmitirlos a un servidor central a trav├®s de redes GPRS o WiFi, utilizando el protocolo MQTT.
+
+## 2. Alcance del Proyecto
+El firmware est├í orientado a la monitorizaci├│n remota de activos, permitiendo:
+- **Geolocalizaci├│n:** Rastreo de ubicaci├│n mediante GNSS (GPS/GLONASS).
+- **Telemetr├¡a de Sensores:** Lectura de sensores anal├│gicos (ADC), digitales (DS18B20) e industriales (Modbus RTU).
+- **Conectividad H├¡brida:** Redundancia de comunicaci├│n mediante GPRS (celular) y WiFi.
+- **Gesti├│n Remota:** Configuraci├│n y actualizaci├│n de firmware (FOTA) de manera remota.
+- **Almacenamiento Local:** Buffering de datos en memoria no vol├ítil cuando no hay conectividad.
+
+## 3. Especificaciones T├®cnicas
+
+### 3.1 Hardware Soportado
+- **MCU:** ESP32 (Dual Core, WiFi/BLE).
+- **M├│dem:** SIMCom SIM7000G (LTE CAT-M1 / NB-IoT / GPRS / GNSS).
+
+### 3.2 Funcionalidades Clave
+
+#### Conectividad y Protocolos
+- **MQTT:** Protocolo principal para env├¡o de datos y recepci├│n de comandos.
+- **Modo H├¡brido:** Prioriza la conexi├│n WiFi cuando est├í disponible; cambia autom├íticamente a GPRS en caso contrario.
+- **Buffering (Flash):** Almacenamiento temporal de paquetes en memoria SPIFFS/LittleFS ante cortes de conexi├│n para evitar p├®rdida de datos.
+
+#### Sensores e Interfaces
+- **ADC (Anal├│gico):**
+  - Lectura de 3 canales anal├│gicos.
+  - Configuraci├│n de filtros (umbral m├¡nimo), factores de calibraci├│n (pendiente/offset) y alisado (promedio m├│vil).
+- **Modbus RTU (RS485):**
+  - Maestro Modbus para interrogar dispositivos esclavos industriales.
+  - Configuraci├│n din├ímica de tramas de consulta (ID, Funci├│n, Direcci├│n, Cantidad).
+- **Sensores Dedicados:** Soporte nativo para sensor de temperatura DS18B20.
+- **Expansora I/O:** Comunicaci├│n con placas expansoras v├¡a puerto serial secundario.
+
+#### Gesti├│n del Dispositivo
+- **Modo AP (Access Point):** Servidor web integrado para configuraci├│n inicial (WiFi, par├ímetros MQTT) activable mediante bot├│n f├¡sico.
+- **Comandos Seriales/Remotos:** Interfaz de l├¡nea de comandos (CLI) completa para configuraci├│n en tiempo real (ver `comandos.MD`).
+- **FOTA:** Actualizaci├│n de firmware v├¡a HTTP.
+- **Watchdog:** Mecanismos de reinicio autom├ítico ante fallos de software o m├│dem.
+
+## 4. Arquitectura de Datos
+El sistema reporta datos en formato JSON a trav├®s de t├│picos MQTT espec├¡ficos:
+- `DVL/LILY-GO/<ID>`: T├│pico principal de telemetr├¡a.
+- `DVL/LILY-GO/<ID>/ADC`: Reportes espec├¡ficos de entradas anal├│gicas.
+
+Los paquetes incluyen timestamp, ubicaci├│n, e informaci├│n de sensores seg├║n la configuraci├│n habilitada (`EN_SENSOR`, `EN_SERIAL`, `EN_MODBUS`).

-MokoSensorData BLEMokoScanner::getLatestData() {

+// Devuelve el siguiente dispositivo de la cola y lo remueve
+bool BLEMokoScanner::getNextDevice(MokoSensorData *data) {
+  if (deviceQueue.empty()) {
+    newDataAvailable = false;
+    return false;
+  }
+
+  *data = deviceQueue.front();
+  deviceQueue.erase(deviceQueue.begin());
+
+  if (deviceQueue.empty()) {
+    newDataAvailable = false;
+  }
+  return true;
 }
 
 bool BLEMokoScanner::hasNewData() { return newDataAvailable; }

+1. Overview
+
+The MOKO Door Sensor, integrated with data logging and real-time status alerts, is a BLE 4.2 based wireless Beacon
+to detects when doors open or close for powerful visibility.
+With precise sensors and an easy-to-install design, the door monitor provides powerful visibility and improved
+security to both roll-up and swing (i.e., hinge) doors without any complicated wiring or cables. It also delivers
+enhanced visibility of trailer and container operations and improved cargo security , which enables fleet operators to
+know if the cargo area is being accessed as scheduled and react quickly to unexpected openings.
+Thanks to its outstanding 2600mAh large battery, the door monitor could run for 8+ years to eliminate the
+maintenance costs.
+
+Figure 1: Typical application scenario of DOOR SENSOR
+
+                                                                                       6
+
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2023 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                                        Door Sensor User Manual V1.1
+

+3. How the DOOR SENSOR works?
+
+The DOOR SENSOR is based on hall sensor, to detects hall sensor status respect to magnet (i.e., door closed/open).
+The typical logical is to detects the hall sensor status, and then upload the status to the gateway node through BLE
+advertisement. So, we will describe door open/close status detection and BLE advertisement separately.
+
+3.1 Door open/close status detection
+
+In DOOR SENSOR, we have integrated with the hall sensor (need to be applied together with magnet accessory) to
+monitor the doorÔÇÖs open/close status.
+You can refer to ÔÇ£chapter 4.1 ÔÇô How to install DOOR SENSORÔÇØ to install the DOOR SENSOR as well as magnet
+accessory. When magnet is approaching to hall sensor and distance is less than 20mm, hall sensor will output high
+level and device will report ÔÇ£1ÔÇØ in the BLE advertisement to the gateway for processing and delivery to cloud services.
+
+Figure 3: How to check doorÔÇÖs status in the advertisement?
+
+                                                                                      10
+
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2023 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                                        Door Sensor User Manual V1.1
+
+3.2 Bluetooth advertisement
+
+In last chapter, we have mentioned that the DOOR SENSOR is uploading the sensor status or other basic information
+through BLE advertisement, and that basic information can also be configured through MOKO ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP. Before
+introducing the APP user manual, we will guide you to fully understand the advertisement format and content
+parsing rules.
+Regarding of PIR series firmware, there have only one customized advertisement format, which is a combination of
+ADV_IND (Advertising packet) and SCAN_RSP (Scan response packet). In the next we will describe the detailed
+information of these two packets.
+
+     Ô×ó ADV_IND (advertising packet) ---- Mainly to express status of hall sensor, battery voltage, Major, Minor and
+           etc.
+
+     Ô×ó SCAN_RSP (scan response packet) ---- Mainly to express Tx power and device name.
+Below picture showed the example content of Beacon advertisement:
+
+Figure 4: Example content of Beacon advertisement
+
+                                Table 6: Content structure of Customized-PIR advertising packet

+4.2 How to power ON/OFF DOOR SENSOR ?
+
+Regarding of DOOR SENSOR, you can power on/off it through the mechanical button with below operations:
+Ô×ó Power ON: Long press button for more than 3 seconds, and red LED will keep blinking for 2 seconds.
+
+Ô×ó Power OFF: Long press button for more than 3 seconds, and red LED will keep solid for 2 seconds.
+
+Figure 6: How to power on/off DOOR SENSOR?

+4.4 How to restore factory settings ?
+
+There have two ways to restore factory settings.
+Ô×ó ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP (Software reset): Connect DOOR SENSOR with ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP and then execute ÔÇ£Remote resetÔÇØ
+
+      operations to finish the software reset.
+Ô×ó Power Button (Software reset): In power off status, long press button for more than 10s (during this period, LED
+
+      will blink to indicate the device ON, please ignore and keep long press) and then release when you see the LED
+      solid lightening, to finish the software reset.
+
+Remark: Software reset will not reset connection password.

+5. MOKO PIR APP user guidance
+
+This user guidance for ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP is based on version 1.0.3 or above (only android application available now), and
+the contents of this user guidance are subject to change without prior notice for further improvement. For latest APP,
+please download from Google play store.
+Remark: MOKO Door Sensor is working with ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ series firmware, so it is compatible with ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP. And
+you can ignore other sensor information in the APP and only focus on the Door Sensor information regarding of
+Door Sensor product.
+
+5.1 Discover your Door Sensor
+
+The smartphone with ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP installed can be as a Bluetooth Center to scan and discover the advertisement
+packets of Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE) Beacons, and it will provide multiple strategies to filter the Bluetooth packets
+and that can help you to capture the required BLE peripherals directly and quickly.
+
+5.1.1 Scanning your Door Sensor
+
+When entering into the ÔÇ£SCANNING PREVIEWÔÇØ interface, the SCANNER will start scanning and list all surrounding
+advertising Bluetooth LE peripherals which integrated with MOKO PIR series firmware, and you can stop scanning
+manually by pressing scanning button ÔÇ£ ÔÇ£.
+In this SCANNER interface, you will see device advertisement preview to recognize your Beacon device. To distinguish
+specific type of Beacons separately, only Door Sensor can be shown in the APP preview interface and others will be
+filtered automatically. Below picture shows the default scanning preview of MOKO DOOR SENSOR device.
+
+Figure 8: Default scanning preview of DOOR SENSOR
+
+5.1.2 Filtering your Door Sensor
+
+To find your Door Sensor quickly, we have provided multiple strategies to filter among mass Beacon devices, users
+may narrow your device list by BLE name, MAC address and RSSI value through ÔÇ£Edit FilterÔÇØ option.
+

+BLE Name  Allow to input 1-10 characters.

+5.2 Connect with your Door Sensor
+
+For Door Sensors, if the status is connectable, then it will shows ÔÇ£CONNECTÔÇØ button in the ÔÇ£SCANNING PREVIEWÔÇØ
+interface. You can follow below steps to connect with your Beacon device:
+Step 1: Press ÔÇ£CONNECTÔÇØ button, red LED will blink 2seconds rapidly.
+Step 2: Input connection password and then press ÔÇ£OKÔÇØ button, red LED will blink 2seconds rapidly again.
+
+Notice ÔÇô Default password in MOKO standard firmware is ÔÇ£Moko4321ÔÇØ.
+
+5.3 Getting your Door Sensor to broadcast
+
+Once device connected and the main screen of the ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP appears, including ADVERTISEMENT interface,
+SETTING interface and DEVICE interface.
+Remark: If there are no write/read operations within 2 minutes after device connected, then it will be disconnected
+automatically to save power consumption.
+
+                                                                                      16
+
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2023 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                                        Door Sensor User Manual V1.1

+
+5.3.1 Setting up advertisement content
+
+After you connected with device, then you can go to ÔÇ£ADVERTISEMENT >>> Adv contentÔÇØ part to configure the
+corresponding advertisement content. Details are as below.
+
+Figure 11: How to configure advertising content?
+     Ô×ó Major ---- You can define the type of value as you want in practical scenario, such as dividing region, group
+           or etc. Configuration range: 0-65535
+     Ô×ó Minor ---- You can define the type of value as you want in practical scenario, such as specific room, device or
+           etc. Configuration range: 0-65535
+     Ô×ó Device name ---- ASCII characters within 10 bytes.
+     Ô×ó Serial ID ---- Serial ID from 0 to 99999.
+
+Remark: Complete local name is a combination of ÔÇ£Device nameÔÇØ and ÔÇ£Serial IDÔÇØ. For instance, if you have set ÔÇ£PIRÔÇØ
+as device name, and ÔÇ£00001ÔÇØ as serial ID, then it will show ÔÇ£PIR00001ÔÇØ in the preview interface. Or if you checked with
+ÔÇ£nRF ConnectÔÇØ APP, the complete local name will show ÔÇ£PIR00001ÔÇØ.
+
+                                                                                      17
+
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2023 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.

+
+5.3.2 Setting up advertising parameters
+
+After you connected with device, you can also go to ÔÇ£ADVERTISEMENT >>> Adv parametersÔÇØ part to configure the
+corresponding advertisement parameters. Details are as below.
+
+Figure 12: How to configure advertising parameters?
+     Ô×ó Adv Interval ---- The time between advertising packets. Configuration range: 100ms ÔÇô 10000ms.
+     Ô×ó RSSI@1m ---- Value that declares to receiving devices what the power should be at 1m distance from the
+           Beacon. Configuration range: -100dBm ÔÇô 0dBm.
+     Ô×ó Tx Power ---- Output power in number of dBm. Configuration level: -40/-20/-16/-12/-8/-4/0/+4 dBm.
+
+5.4 Access to Hall Sensor status
+
+Currently we have adopted ÔÇ£door/window intrusion alarmÔÇØ functions for ÔÇ£MOKO Door SensorÔÇØ products. For the door
+sensor status monitoring, you can go to ÔÇ£SETTING >>> PIR & HallÔÇØ interface for further configurations.

+5.5 Modify connection password
+
+In DOOR SENSOR firmware, ÔÇ£Moko4321ÔÇØ was set as default connection password. You can try to modify your
+BeaconÔÇÖs connection password by just tapping ÔÇ£SETTING >>> modify passwordÔÇØ option, and then input the password
+as you want.
+Remark: Connection password must be 8 characters in length.
+
+5.6 Switch your Door Sensor to unconnectable
+
+In some cases, you will need to disable the Beacon connectable property, to avoid high power consumption caused
+by deliberate connections. So, you can disable the connectable status in ÔÇ£SETTING >>> ConnectableÔÇØ option. After
+disabled, device will not be connected by master BLE device anymore unless reset the Beacon.
+
+Figure 14: Switch your Door Sensor to unconnectable status
+
+5.7 Disable button power off function
+
+Normally the button is used for power on/off purpose, however in some specific cases, there will need to disable the
+button function in order to avoid deliberate operations, so logically there also should have the corresponding switch
+to enable/disable it. You can go to ÔÇ£SETTING >>> Button Power OFFÔÇØ to disable the button power off functions.
+
+     Ô×ó Button power off ----- You will not be able to turn off the device through mechanical button once disabled.
+
+Figure 15: Disable ÔÇ£button power offÔÇØ function
+
+5.8 Remote turn off your Door Sensor
+
+In some cases that mechanical button function should be disabled to avoid intentional shut down situations, then we
+will need to remote shut down function for backup.
+Under this circumstance, you can remote turn off your Beacon by just tapping ÔÇ£DEVICE >>> Remote Power OFFÔÇØ
+option, then it will give prompt to you to confirm the shut down operations.
+
+Figure 16: Remote power off your Door Sensor
+
+           Notice ÔÇô Please make sure that device have mechanical button, or other ways to turn on before you do
+           remote shut down operations.
+
+                                                                                      19
+
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2023 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                                        Door Sensor User Manual V1.1
+
+5.9 Remote reset your Door Sensor
+
+In some cases that mechanical button function should be disabled or without mechanical button, to avoid intentional
+reset situations, then we will need remote reset function for backup. You can remote reset your Beacon by just
+tapping ÔÇ£DEVICE >>> Remote resetÔÇØ option, then it will give prompt to you to confirm the reset operations.
+Remark: Remote reset operations will not change the password to factory reset status. If you want to totally reset the
+Beacon with password restore as well, then you may need to do hardware reset operations.
+
+5.10 Check your device information
+
+In some cases, you will need to know the firmware version of Beacon, or product model and etc. You can access to
+ÔÇ£DeviceÔÇØ interface to check and confirm that essential information, which including battery voltage, running time
+since power on, MAC address and etc.
+
+5.11 Upgrade your deviceÔÇÖs firmware
+
+The Beacon can realize OTA firmware update through DFU operations. In the next we will describe the detailed steps
+based on android ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP.
+Step 1: Copy the upgrade package to a folder in the root directory of the smartphone.
+Step 2: Connect with device and go to ÔÇ£SETTINGÔÇØ interface, press ÔÇ£OTA DFUÔÇØ option and then file manager route of
+
+         smartphone will be prompted.
+Step 3: Choose the required upgrade firmware package and the upgrade process will start automatically. Wait for
+
+         the upgrade to complete, APP will disconnect with Beacon after upgrade completed successfully.
+Remark: Please contact with our sales team for latest firmware OTA package if need.
+
+Figure 17: Firmware upgrade workflow of ÔÇ£MK PIRÔÇØ APP (Android)
+
+5.12 Obtain log file from Android APP
+
+Sometimes when testing with our APP, there may occurred some issues that cannot be reproduced in MOKO side,
+then we will need you to help obtain the log file from android APP and then provide for us to further analysis.

+1. About IN100 Chip Solution Features
+
+The main features of products based on IN100 chip solutions include the following aspects:
+´ü¼ Bluetooth Low Energy 5.3 Compliant
+´ü¼ Support iBeacon, Eddysone(UID/TLM), Customized frame
+´ü¼ Supports a maximum of two broadcast channels
+´ü¼ Support Continuous advertising mode or Event-trigger advertising mode (button trigger)
+
+     ´ü« UT1 / UT1P - support continuous advertising mode and button trigger mode
+     ´ü« M1I - support continuous advertising mode;
+     ´ü« M4I - support continuous advertising mode;
+     ´ü« N1 Stamp - support continuous advertising mode
+     ´ü« N2 - support continuous advertising mode
+´ü¼ The broadcast parameters of the device can be configured only before the device leave the factory, and cannot
+     be configured again after the device leave the factory
+´ü¼ Unconnectable (The device does not support the connection of other Bluetooth devices)
+´ü¼ Low cost and high performance
+´ü¼ Enhanced privacy mode support
+´ü¼ 4KB eFuse memory & 4KB SRAM
+´ü¼ Programmable TX output power. N2&N1 Stamp&M1I&M4I support up to +4dBm; UT1 support up to 0dBm.
+´ü¼ Integrated low leakage LDO, Single cell 1.5V/3V battery support
+ Sub-uW power consumption for multi-year operation on a tiny battery, Sleep mode < 650nA with 32kHz RC ON
+
+                                                                                       4
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                              IN100 Solution Beacon User Guide V1.3

+2. Bluetooth Broadcast Data Format
+
+2.1 Default Setting
+
+The IN100 Series tags are used in the same configuration, the broadcast parameters are as follows:
+
+ SLOT1
+
+ Adv type --NanoBeacon (MOKO Custom)
+ Adv interval -- 3000ms
+ Adv Tx Power -- 0dBm
+ Adv mode -- Continuous
+
+You can refer to the tables below for the actual data format of the device.
+
+                                 Advertisement Data (NanoBeacon)
+
+Byte offset  Field               Example value        Descriptions
+0            Data length
+1            Data type           0x06                 AD length of Flags content
+2-6          Device name
+7            Data length         0x09                 AD type: Flags
+8            Data type
+9 - 10       Manufacturer ID     0x4e 42 54 41 47     Device name : NBTAG
+11           Adv type
+12           Battery voltage     0x12                 AD length of NanoBeacon content
+
+13 - 14      Beacon temperature  0xff                 Manufacturer Spec
+
+15 - 18      SEC_CNT             0x05 05              Manufacturer ID
+
+19           Trigger status      0x00                 0x00 -- Normal Adv type
+20-25        MAC address
+                                 0x60                 (Little-endian), uint: 0.03125V
+                                                      Content Parse: 0x60 >>>96 -- > 96├ù0.03125V = 3V
+
+                                 0x30 0A              (Little-endian), uint: 0.01Ôäâ
+                                 0x8B 7C 3B 00        Content Parse: 0x0A 30>>>2610 -- > 2610├ù0.01Ôäâ = 26.1Ôäâ
+
+                                                      (Little-endian), SEC_CNT is a 1 second resolution counter that
+                                                      represents time since beacon power-up or reboot. If this value is
+                                                      reset (e.g. on a reboot), the ADV count field must also be reset.
+                                                      4 bytes, Content parse: 0x00 3B 7C 8B>>>3,898,507, that is
+                                                      3,898,507s
+
+                                 0xDD                 Fixed value: 0xDD
+
+                                 0x46 4D A3 71 3E FF  (Little-endian), Content parse:
+                                                      Beacon MAC address: FF:3E:71:A3:4D:46
+
+                                                             5
+                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                           IN100 Solution Beacon User Guide V1.3
+
+3. How to power ON/OFF the tag
+
+3.1 How to power ON/OFF the tags?
+
+Please check the table below for a description of the features
+
+Product model  Power on                                                                 Power off
+
+       M1I     The device is powered on by default. Open the device casing Power-off function can be achieved
+       M4I
+   UT1/UT1P    and remove the insulating sheet to power it on.             by installing an insulating sheet.
+    N1 Stamp
+        N2     The device is powered on by default. Remove the insulating  Power-off function can be achieved
+               sheet to power it on.                                       by installing an insulating sheet.
+
+               The product is powered off by default. Tear along the easy  Once powered on, it does not support
+               tear line to power on the product.                          powering off again.
+
+               The device is powered on by default. Remove the insulating  Power-off function can be achieved
+               sheet to power it on.                                       by installing an insulating sheet.
+
+               Peel off the release paper on the back of the product to    Not support
+               power on the device.
+
+3.2 How to confirm the tag is powered on?
+
+You can determine if the device is operational by using the BeaconX Pro app to scan for its broadcast. If the broadcast
+from the tag's specified MAC address is detected, it indicates the device is powered on. If no broadcast is detected
+after an extended period of scanning, it can be inferred that the device is not yet powered on.
+
+Step1. Open the BeaconX Pro app, with app version V2.0.7 or higher, and select the ÔÇ£NanoBeacon seriesÔÇØ, then you
+will enter the scanning page.
+
+Step2. Click on ÔÇ£Edit FilterÔÇØ to enter the scan filter page. Choose the QR code scanning function and use your phone
+camera to scan the QR code on the product. The QR code contains the MAC address information . Once the QR code
+
+                                                                                       6
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             IN100 Solution Beacon User Guide V1.3
+
+is successfully recognized, the MAC address information of this device will automatically populate the scan filter box.
+Click 'Apply' and wait for a moment. If the app's scan page detects the broadcast information from the corresponding
+device, it indicates that the device has been successfully powered on and is operating normally.
+
+                                                                                       7
+                                                                        Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                            IN100 Solution Beacon User Guide V1.3
+
+4. Advertising Data Configuration
+
+The hardware based on the IN100 chip solution support configure the advertising data and advertising parameters of
+the Bluetooth beacon according to customerÔÇÖs application requirements through the configuration tool to achieve
+the purpose of different broadcast functions.
+MOKO can also provide pure hardware equipment, which can be configured by the customer, or MOKO can configure
+the parameters as customerÔÇÖs requirements for verification.
+The GUI of the configuration tool is shown in the below picture.
+
+Note: The broadcast parameters of the device can be configured only once before the device leave the factory, and
+cannot be configured again after the device leave the factory
+The Bluetooth beacon supports configure the following functions and parameters:
+´ü¼ The Bluetooth beacon supports up to two advertising slots sets which means beacon can broadcasts different
+
+     advertising packets separately.
+´ü¼ The Bluetooth beacon supports two commonly used advertising data formats.
+
+     ´âÿ iBeacon: Apple proprietary Beacon advertising frame format based on Bluetooth standard advertising
+        (The parameters of UUID/ Major/ Minor/ Measured Tx Power in the iBeacon data format support configure)
+
+     ´âÿ Eddystone(UID/TLM): Google proprietary Beacon advertising frame format based on the service data of the
+           Bluetooth standard advertising.
+
+     ´âÿ Custom: the advertising data in a custom user-defined format as customerÔÇÖs application requirements
+´ü¼ The Bluetooth beacon supports configure the button trigger function which means the device support advertising
+
+     the specified slotÔÇÖs broadcast after trigger event occurs.
+

+2. Content parsing of Beacon advertisement
+
+Before introducing the APP user manual, we will guide you to fully understand the Beacon advertisement format and
+content parsing rules. In terms of the MOKO ÔÇ£BXP-SÔÇØ series firmware, which is especially design for asset tracking, it
+contains several kinds of advertisement frame type which include standard format and customized format, that is:
+ ´âÿ Google EddystoneTM - UID
+ ´âÿ Google EddystoneTM - URL
+ ´âÿ Google EddystoneTM - TLM (Unencrypted)
+ ´âÿ Customized advertisement frame ÔÇô ÔÇ£iBeaconÔÇØ
+ ´âÿ Customized advertisement frame - ÔÇ£Sensor infoÔÇØ
+To improve the readability of the screen formats, this document presents:
+
+  - The fixed Bluetooth fields highlighted in blue.
+  - The variable Bluetooth fields highlighted in orange.
+  - User-defined/configurable fields highlighted in green.
+  - Reserved fields highlighted in red.
+The screenshots have been taken on the ÔÇ£nRF ConnectÔÇØ application, developed by Nordic Semiconductors.
+

+2.1 Google Eddystone ÔÇô UID
+
+In terms of UID advertisement frame type, this is exactly complied with Google Eddystone regulations, and you can
+refer to below link for more information: https://github.com/google/eddystone/tree/master/eddystone-uid
+
+Below table described the detailed content structure and frame example of UID advertisement:
+
+                                           Eddystone - UID
+
+                 Frame format                                            Frame example
+
+             01  Length: 0x02
+
+             02  Type: 0x01
+
+             03  Data: 0x06
+
+             04  Length: 0x03
+
+             05  Type: 0x03
+
+             06  Eddystone_UUID_LSB: 0xAA
+
+             07  Eddystone_UUID_MSB: 0xFE
+
+             08  Length: 0x17
+
+             09  Type: 0x16
+
+             10  Eddystone_UUID_LSB: 0xAA
+
+             11  Eddystone_UUID_MSB: 0xFE
+
+             12  Frame type UUID: 0x00
+
+Frame bytes  13  RSSI@0m: 0x00
+
+             14  Namespace ID[0]
+
+             15  Namespace ID[1]
+
+             16  Namespace ID[2]
+
+             17  Namespace ID[3]
+
+             18  Namespace ID[4]
+
+             19  Namespace ID[5]
+
+             20  Namespace ID[6]
+
+             21  Namespace ID[7]
+
+             22  Namespace ID[8]
+
+             23  Namespace ID[9]
+
+             24  Instance ID[0]

+             25  Instance ID[1]
+
+             26  Instance ID[2]
+
+             27  Instance ID[3]
+
+             28  Instance ID[4]
+
+             29  Instance ID[5]

+2.4 Customized frame ÔÇô ÔÇ£iBeaconÔÇØ
+
+This Customized iBeacon advertisement frame is combined with two parts, of which one is standard APPLE iBeacon
+format which comply with APPLE iBeacon regulations, another one is a customized scan response packet.
+
+What the purpose of adding extra scan response packet is just displaying more information on iOS APP, as APP cannot
+get the detail information from APPLE iBeacon advertisement due to iOS system restrictions. For APPLE iBeacon
+regulations, you can refer to the link: https://developer.apple.com/ibeacon/Getting-Started-with-iBeacon.pdf.
+
+Below table described the detailed content structure and frame example of Customized-iBeacon advertisement:
+
+                 MOKO Customized - iBeacon
+
+                 Standard iBeacon packet                 Scan response packet
+                                                              Length: 0x02
+             01  Length: 0x02                                   Type: 0x0A
+                                                             Tx Power: 0x00
+             02  Type: 0x01                                   Length: 0x1A
+                                                                Type: 0x16
+             03  Data: 0x06
+                                          Service data UUID_LSB: 0xAB (MOKO defined)
+             04  Length: 0x1A             Service data UUID_MSB: 0xFE (MOKO defined)
+
+             05  Type: 0xFF                      Frame type: 0x50 (MOKO defined)
+                                                             RSSI@1m: 0xC5
+             06  Apple CIN_LSB: 0x4C
+                                                           ADV interval: 0x0A
+             07  Apple CIN_MSB: 0x00                             UUID[0]
+                                                                 UUID[1]
+             08  Beacon type: 0x02                               UUID[2]
+                                                                 UUID[3]
+             09  Data size: 0x15                                 UUID[4]
+                                                                 UUID[5]
+             10  UUID[0]                                         UUID[6]
+                                                                 UUID[7]
+             11  UUID[1]                                         UUID[8]
+                                                                 UUID[9]
+             12  UUID[2]                                        UUID[10]
+                                                                UUID[11]
+Frame bytes  13  UUID[3]                                        UUID[12]
+                                                                UUID[13]
+             14  UUID[4]                                        UUID[14]
+                                                                UUID[15]
+             15  UUID[5]                                         Major[0]
+                                                                 Major[1]
+             16  UUID[6]                                         Minor[0]
+                                                                 Minor[0]
+             17  UUID[7]                                         Not used
+
+             18  UUID[8]
+
+             19  UUID[9]
+
+             20  UUID[10]

+2.5 Customized frame ÔÇô ÔÇ£Sensor infoÔÇØ
+
+This ÔÇ£Sensor infoÔÇØ advertisement frame is customized by MOKO and also combined with two parts which is
+ÔÇ£advertising packetÔÇØ and ÔÇ£scan response packetÔÇØ.
+
+     ´âÿ ADV_IND (advertising packet) ----Mainly to express the tag ID, sensor status, trigger event counts, battery
+           voltage and acceleration data.
+
+     ´âÿ SCAN_RSP (scan response packet) ----Mainly to express the device name.
+
+Below table described the detailed content structure and frame example of Customized-Sensor info advertisement:
+
+                 MOKO Customized - Sensor info
+
+                 Advertisement packet                               Scan response packet
+                                                                        Length: Ôëñ 0x15
+             01  Length: 0x02                                             Type: 0x09
+                                                                       Device name[0]

+                                                                       Device name[2]

+                                                                       Device name[8]
+             07  Service data UUID_MSB: 0xEA (MOKO defined)            Device name[9]
+                                                                       Device name[10]
+             08  Frame type UUID: 0x80 (MOKO defined)                  Device name[11]
+                                                                       Device name[12]
+             09                         Sensor status                  Device name[13]
+                 (Magnetic/ Motion/ Temperature/ Humidity/ Flash)      Device name[14]
+                                                                       Device name[15]
+             10  MAGNET_CNT_LSB                                        Device name[16]
+                                                                       Device name[17]
+             11  MAGNET_CNT_MSB                                        Device name[18]
+                                                                       Device name[19]
+             12  MOTION_CNT_LSB
+                                                                           Not used
+Frame bytes  13  MOTION_CNT_MSB                                            Not used
+                                                                           Not used
+             14  Accel.Dat_ X axis (mg) LSB                                Not used
+                                                                           Not used
+             15  Accel.Dat_ X axis (mg) MSB                                Not used
+                                                                           Not used
+             16  Accel.Dat_ Y axis (mg) LSB                                Not used
+                                                                           Not used
+             17  Accel.Dat_ Y axis (mg) MSB
+
+             18  Accel.Dat_ Z axis (mg) LSB
+
+             19  Accel.Dat_ Z axis (mg) MSB
+
+             20  Temperature.Dat (Ôäâ) LSB
+
+             21  Temperature.Dat (Ôäâ) MSB
+
+             22  Humidity.Dat (%RH) LSB
+
+             23  Humidity.Dat (%RH) MSB

+             24 Battery percentage(1%) / Battery voltage (1mV)_LSB
+             25 Battery percentage(1%) / Battery voltage (1mV)_MSB

+             26  Tag ID[0]

+                                                                           MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+                                   Received frame : MOKO Customized - Sensor info
+
+               [Bit0]: Magnetic sensor working status                   [Bit0] = 0 : Magnetic is approaching (RFU)
+
+               [Bit1]: Motion sensor working status                     [Bit1] = 0 : Device is in static
+
+Sensor status  [Bit2]: Motion sensor equipped status       0011 1100ÔÇÖb  [Bit2] = 1 : Motion sensor is equipped in the device
+               [Bit3]: Temperature sensor equipped status     (0x1C)    [Bit3] = 1 : Temperature sensor is equipped in the device
+               [Bit4]: Humidity sensor equipped status                  [Bit4] = 1 : Humidity sensor is equipped in the device
+
+               [Bit5]: Flash equipped status                            [Bit5] = 0 : Flash is not equipped in the device
+
+               [Bit6-7]: Reserved                                       [Bit6-7]: Reserved
+
+               MAGNET_CNT ADV interval                     0x0000 = 0 (Times of magnetic triggered) (RFU)
+
+               MOTION_CNT                                  0x0002 = 2 (Times of motion triggered)
+
+               Accel.Data_X axis                           0x003A = 58mg
+
+               Accel.Data_Y axis                           0x0076 = 118mg
+
+               Accel.Data_Z axis                           0x03A7 = 935mg
+
+               Temperature Data                            Sampling temperature in 2 bytes (Signed integer), unit: 0.1Ôäâ/digit.
+                                                           Example content parse: 0x00 C8(HEX)>>>200(DEC)>>>20.0Ôäâ
+
+               Humidity Data                               Sampling humidity in 2 bytes (Unsigned integer), unit: 0.1%/digit.
+                                                           Example content parse: 0x01 37(HEX)>>>311(DEC)>>>31.1%RH
+
+Battery percentage(1%/unit) / Battery voltage (1mv/unit)   If value greater than 100, it represents the battery voltage.
+                                                           For example, 0x0CCC = 3276mV.
+
+                                                           If value less than or equal to 100, it represents the battery percentage.
+                                                           For example, 0x0064 = 100%.
+
+               Tag ID                                      0x000001
+
+               Device Name                                 0x4D4B2053656E736F72 = MK Sensor
+
+                                   Table 5: Explanation of format parsing ÔÇô Sensor info
+
+                                                                        16
+                                                   Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.

+3. APP user guidance
+
+Well, you have already familiar with the advertisement format and well to know how to parse the content in the
+advertisement, now we will guide you to know the MK Sensor Series APP and letÔÇÖs do some configuration on the Tag.
+For the latest APP, please search ÔÇ£MK SensorÔÇØ(Android) and download from Google play store, search ÔÇ£BeaconX
+ProÔÇØ(IOS) and download from Apple store.
+
+3.1 Discover your tag
+
+The smartphone with ÔÇ£BeaconX ProÔÇØ APP (Android or iOS) installed can be as a Bluetooth Center to scan and discover
+the advertisement packets of Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE) Beacons, and it will provide multiple strategies to filter the
+Bluetooth packets and that can help you to capture the required BLE peripherals directly and quickly.
+

+         Sensor seriesÔÇØoption.

+3.2 Connect with your tag

+correct connection password and press ÔÇ£OKÔÇØ button, You can successfully enter the device configuration interface.
+Notice : Default password in MOKO standard firmware is ÔÇ£Moko4321ÔÇØ.

+3.3 Know your tagÔÇÖs advertising logic
+
+Before setting up your tagÔÇÖs advertisement, you may need to understand the basic advertising logic and working
+flowchart of the tag. With the integration of ultra-long advertising interval (standby duration) feature , in order to
+guarantee the efficiency of connections based on ultra-long advertising interval mode, we have integrated a constant
+advertising cycle which is called configuration period when device boot up.
+
+The configuration period will last for 30 seconds and then device will start working (advertising period) by following
+the rules you preset in the advertisement SLOTs.
+
+Advertising Period    Device status  Overall advertising logic
+Configuration period   Connectable                                  Default Configurations
+
+ Advertising period                                         Adv interval ÔÇô 1000ms (non-configurable)
+                                                              Adv duration ÔÇô 30s (non-configurable)
+
+                      Connectable      Low-power mode ÔÇô disable (configurable)
+                                         Adv interval ÔÇô 1000ms (configurable)
+                                           Adv duration ÔÇôÔÇô 10s (configurable)
+
+                                       Trigger function ÔÇô disable (configurable)
+
+                      Table 6: Different adverting periods in overall advertising logic
+
+                      Figure 5: Overall advertising logic by default parameters
+

+3.4 Access to SLOT configurations
+
+Once device connected, the main screen of the APP will display the SLOT interface. This interface is used to configure
+broadcast parameters of the device.
+There have three advertisement slots can be configured separately in SLOT interface, and different device type will
+show different advertisement frame.
+
+                                    Figure 6: Configurations of advertisement SLOT interface
+Remark: The tag can be configured with a maximum of three SLOTs. Six advertising types are supported. No data
+indicating that the SLOT is disabled.
+
+               Notice ÔÇô It is not recommended to configure all 3 slots as No data, that means the device will not
+               broadcast any advertisement unless user reset the Beacon.
+
+3.4.1 Setting your tagÔÇÖs advertisement SLOT
+
+You can configure a certain frame type on different SLOT, each SLOT can be same frame type or different frame type.
+Please refer to Chapter 2 : Content parsing of Beacon advertisement to know the parameters of the Adv content.
+
+                                                                                      20
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+                      Figure 7: Parameters and ADV process diagram of advertisement SLOT interface
+Low-power mode : The device follows a broadcast-sleep cycle operation mode. Initially, it broadcasts for a set period
+of time(ADV Duration) and then enters a low-power sleep state(Standby period). Upon receiving a trigger or
+reaching the scheduled wake-up time(Standby duration), it wakes up to broadcasts for a set period of time again.
+After completing the task, the device returns to sleep mode, minimizing energy consumption while remaining ready
+to operate when needed.
+´ü« If the Low-power mode is enable, the ÔÇ£Standby durationÔÇØ parameter becomes configurable, representing the
+
+     duration the device will sleep after broadcasting for a period.
+´ü« If the Low-power mode is disable (equivalent to ÔÇ£Standby durationÔÇØ set to 0), and the ÔÇ£Adv durationÔÇØ parameter
+
+     will become a invalid parameter (The entire broadcasting cycle will also have no concept of Standby period).
+
+3.4.2 Setting your tagÔÇÖs trigger function
+
+Trigger function is designed for some emergency states switching or some specific defined use cases, which include
+Motion trigger, Temperature trigger and Humidity trigger. You can identify the different custom defined event
+through different advertising status before and after triggered.
+Click the ÔÇ£TriggerÔÇØ option, then it will automatically jump to the trigger function configuration guide interface.
+Step 1: Initial Setting. In this step, you can configure parameters for tags to broadcast after the trigger event occurs.
+
+         The ÔÇ£TriggerÔÇØ button must be enabled to processed to the next step.
+
+                                   Figure 8: Parameters for the step1 of the trigger function.
+
+                                                                                      21
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+         ´âÿ Trigger button: This button must be turned on, otherwise you will not be able to proceed to the next step.
+         ´âÿTrigger type: Motion detection / Temperature detection / Humidity detection / Magnetic detection
+         ´âÿ Trigger event: Indicates the conditions for determining the occurrence of an event. If the event occurs,
+
+               broadcasting will be carried out according to the broadcasting parameters specified in Step 2.
+         Note:
+         ´âÿ Static verify period: The stationary detection duration is only applicable to the "Motion detection" type
+
+               and indicates the condition for determining the occurrence of a stationary event.If Trigger event is
+               "Device start moving", The duration for determining stillness dictates the broadcast duration after a
+               motion-triggered event. The broadcast duration following a single motion trigger will always be shorter
+               than the stillness determination duration.
+         ´âÿ Lock Event Occurs ADV Duration: This function is only applicable to the ÔÇ£Temperature detection /
+               Humidity detection / Magnetic detectionÔÇØ. If the device quickly returns to a state where the triggering
+               condition is no longer met after initially satisfying the triggering condition, it can only broadcast for a
+               short duration, or might not broadcast at all. The Locked ADV function ensures that, in such cases, the
+               set post-trigger broadcast duration is fully executed, regardless of changes in the triggering condition. If
+               the ÔÇ£Total adv durationÔÇØ in step2 is set to 0, the Lock Event Occurs adv duration will default to a locked
+               broadcast of 5 seconds. (Please refer to the Chapter 3.4.3 Lock Event Occurs ADV Duration for a more
+               detailed understanding of this feature)
+Step 2: Event occurs setting. In this step, you can configure the advertising parameters of trigger event occurs. The
+         broadcasting parameters configured in this step are related to the ÔÇ£Trigger eventÔÇØ parameters configured in
+         Step 1. For example, if you configure the ÔÇ£Trigger eventÔÇØ as 'Device starts moving', the device will broadcast
+         according to the parameters configured in this step after it starts moving.
+
+                                   Figure 9: Parameters for the step2 of the trigger function.
+
+                                                                                      22
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+Step 3: Before event occurs setting. If you want the device to broadcast before the trigger event occurs, you can
+         enable this feature. In this step, you can set the broadcasting parameters related to the time before the
+         trigger event happens. The state before the trigger event refers to the opposite state of the trigger event you
+         configured in Step 1. For example, if the trigger event is configured as 'device starts moving,' the state before
+         the trigger event occurs refers to the device being stationary; if the trigger event is configured as 'door open,'
+         the state before the trigger event occurs refers to the door being closed.
+
+                                   Figure 10: Parameters for the step3 of the trigger function.
+Once all configuration steps are completed, click the ÔÇ£DoneÔÇØ button. A reminder box will pop up, which mainly
+provides an explanation of the device's broadcasting process. If you confirm there are no issues, click 'OK' and the
+app will send the relevant parameters to configure the device accordingly.
+
+                                 Figure 11: Description of the configured broadcasting process
+You can also refer to Chapter 3.5 How to apply to your scenarios to learn more about detailed application scenarios,
+related configuration parameters and related broadcasting flowchart.
+
+                                                                                      23
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                      MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+3.4.3 Lock Event Occurs ADV Duration
+
+The ÔÇ£Lock Event Occurs ADV DurationÔÇØ function is only applicable to the ÔÇ£Temperature detection / Humidity
+detection / Magnetic detectionÔÇØ. If the device quickly returns to a state where the triggering condition is no longer
+met after initially satisfying the triggering condition, it can only broadcast for a short duration, or might not
+broadcast at all. The Locked ADV function ensures that, in such cases, the set post-trigger broadcast duration is fully
+executed, regardless of changes in the triggering condition.
+
+If the ÔÇ£Total adv durationÔÇØ in step2 is set to 0, the ÔÇ£Lock Event Occurs ADV DurationÔÇØ will default to a locked
+broadcast of 5 seconds. (Please refer to the below to understand the differences in whether this function is enabled)
+
+Differences in the broadcast process when the Lock Event Occurs ADV Duration feature is enabled or disabled
+
+Parameter Description                 Broadcast Process Diagram
+

+3.5.1 Environmental monitoring and analysis
+
+This environmental monitoring mode is commonly useful for application such as cold-chain transport vehicle/ cold
+storage in cold-chain logistics, vaccine transport storage temperature monitoring in medical industry. fire alarm in
+factory or office.
+In these practical scenarios, you must pay more attention to the periodic temperature data, especially the alarm
+information above the temperature threshold, and in order to save battery consumption, you can lower the
+advertisement report frequency when the temperature below the temperature threshold. In addition, the device
+may also need to periodically store temperature data for later analysis.

+3.7 Quick switch for basic features
+
+In current firmware, we have designed multiple function switch for some basic functions. Based on that, you can
+disable/enable these features through the switch, thus adapting to different use cases.
+You can enable/disable these function switch in ÔÇ£SETTING >>> Quick switchÔÇØ section, and below shows the ÔÇ£Quick
+switchÔÇØ interface as well as relevant feature descriptions.
+
+                                          Figure 22: Configurations page of Quick switch
+
+                                                                                      32
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+3.7.1 Switch your Tag to non-connectable status
+
+In some cases, you will need to disable the tagÔÇÖs connectable property, to let it work just as a location tag. So you can
+disable the connectable status in ÔÇ£SETTING >>> Quick switch >>> Connectable statusÔÇØ. After feature disabled, device
+wonÔÇÖt be connected by master BLE device anymore unless reset your tag.
+
+3.7.2 Disable trigger LED indicator
+
+If you preset the motion trigger or magnetic trigger mechanism, the LED will blink for 1 time when it is triggered.
+Considering the invisibility in some specific scenarios or the save of battery consumption, you can access to ÔÇ£SETTING
+>> Quick switch >> Trigger LED indicatorÔÇØ to disable the LED notification in trigger mode.
+
+3.7.3 Disable password verification
+
+You can go to ÔÇ£DEVICE >> Quick switch >> Password verificationÔÇØ to disable password verification. Once password
+verification be disabled, there will be no need to input password when you connect with your tag.
+Remark: If password verification feature was disabled, you will not be able to change password or reset Beacon due
+to security process restrictions.
+
+3.7.4 Autofill Tag ID
+
+As we have described previously, MOKO customized advertisement Sensor info format contained Tag ID.If you want
+the Tag ID and mac address to be the same, you can enable this feature and The Tag ID field of the Sensor info is
+automatically filled with beacon mac address.
+You can access to ÔÇ£DEVICE >> Quick switch >> Tag ID AutofillÔÇØ to enable it and then relevant information which
+included in scan response packet will not be shown in the ÔÇ£SCANNING PREVIEWÔÇØ interface.
+
+3.7.5 Disable/Enable your BeaconÔÇÖs button function
+
+Basically the independent button in the Beacon was designed to realize power off or reset functions, but in some
+specific cases, there will need to disable the button functions in order to avoid intended shutdown or reset operations.
+So logically there also have the switch to enable/disable these functions.
+You can access to SETTING >> Quick switch >> Turn off Beacon by button and SETTING >> Quick switch >> Reset
+Beacon by button to disable/enable these two button control functions.
+
+3.8 Factory reset your Beacon
+
+3.8.1 Remote reset
+
+In some cases that mechanical button function should be disabled, to avoid intentional reset situations. Under this
+circumstance we will need remote reset function for backup. You can remote reset your tag by just connect with your
+tag and tapping ÔÇ£SETTING >>> Reset BeaconÔÇØ option, then it will give prompt to you to confirm the reset operations.
+Remark: Remote reset operations will not change the connection password to factory reset status. If you want to
+totally reset the Beacon with password restore as well, then you may need to do hardware reset operations.
+
+                                                                                      33
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+3.8.2 Hardware reset
+
+If your device is already in a non-connectable state, you will not be able to perform the remote reset operation
+mentioned in the previous section. Then you can try to perform a factory reset on your device using a hardware reset.
+Before performing a hardware reset, please make sure your device is loaded with the appropriate hardware.
+´âÿ Hardware reset by button
+If your device has a external or inner button and the ÔÇ£SETTING >> Quick switch >> Reset Beacon by buttonÔÇØ option is
+turned off, you can try using the button to perform a factory reset. Below is the operation flow:
+
+  Step1: Keep the device power off. If the device is turned on, please press and hold the button for 3 seconds to
+  turn it off.
+  Step2: Press and hold the button for 10 seconds or more, during this operation the device will power on and LED
+  start flashing for 3 seconds.
+  Step3: After long press duration more than 10 seconds, release the button and then press the button again within
+  2 seconds.
+  Step4:The LED on the device will keep solid red for 3 seconds, and then the device will automatically restart. After
+  completing the above steps, you are done restoring factory Settings.
+
+´âÿ Hardware reset by magnet:
+If your device has an inner hall sensor switch and the ÔÇ£SETTING >> Quick switch >> Reset Beacon by buttonÔÇØ option
+is turned off, you can try using the magnet to perform a factory reset. Below is the operation flow:
+
+  Step1: Keep the device power off. If the device is turned on, hold a magnet close to the position marked with a
+  Hall switch sign on the product and keep it there for 3 seconds to turn it off, then remove the magnet and keep it
+  away from the device .
+  Step2: hold the magnet close to the position marked with a Hall switch sign on the product for 10 seconds or more,
+  during this operation the device will power on and LED start flashing for 3 seconds.
+  Step3: After hold the magnet close to Hall switch duration more than 10 seconds, remove the magnet and keep it
+  away from the device and then hold the magnet close to the device again within 2 seconds.
+  Step4:The LED on the device will keep solid red for 3 seconds, and then the device will automatically restart. After
+  completing the above steps, you are done restoring factory Settings.
+The following figure describes the flowchart about the hardware reset operation using button or magnets:
+
+                                     Figure 23: Hardware reset by button/magnet flowchart
+
+                                                                                      34
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+´âÿ Hardware reset by battery:
+In order to avoid the stuck in the dilemma of not being able to restore factory settings due to the hardware design
+without physical buttons, we have provided a universal operation flow to execute the hardware reset.
+
+  Step1: Remove the cover and the battery.
+  Step2: Put the battery in until you see the LED keep blinking for 2 seconds. This LED blinking means that the
+  battery is connected successfully.
+  Step3: When the LED blinking finishes, put the battery in again within 5 seconds, and then repeat the process four
+  more times, removing and replacing the battery. You should see a LED blinking each time you put the battery in,
+  for a total of five blinks. The fifth blinking is different from the previous four and indicates that the device is factory
+  reset successfully.
+
+           1st ÔÇô 4th : LED keep blinking for 2s. This indicates the battery is connected successfully.
+           5th : LED keep solid for 2s. This indicates the device is factory reset successfully.
+  Step4: Replace the cover by aligning the battery room and press down on the cover.
+
+                                             Figure 24: How to hardware reset you Tag
+Remark:
+1. Software reset wonÔÇÖt reset connection password.
+The hardware factory reset feature is enabled by default. If you donÔÇÖt need this function, you can disable it through
+the APP.
+
+3.9 Modify ÔÇ£Connection passwordÔÇØ
+
+In MOKO firmware, password verification is ON by default and ÔÇ£Moko4321ÔÇØ was set as default connection password.
+You can modify your BeaconÔÇÖs connection password by just tapping ÔÇ£SETTING >>> modify passwordÔÇØ option, and
+then input the password as you want.
+Remark: Connection password should not exceed 16 characters in length.
+
+3.10 Remote reminder
+
+Enter this configuration item, where you can control the device's LED lights and buzzer. Please make sure that your
+device is equipped with LED or a buzzer before proceeding.
+
+                                                                                      35
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+3.11 Reset Battery
+
+The device supports broadcasting and displaying the remaining battery level as a percentage. If you have replaced
+the battery with a new one, please connect the device and enter ÔÇ£SETTING>>>Reset BatteryÔÇØ configuration item to
+reset the battery, ensuring that the device can calculate the battery level starting from 100%. Additionally, please
+make sure that the battery you replace is a new one with a full charge.
+
+                                               Figure 25: Reset Battery warning page
+
+3.12 Battery ADV Mode
+
+The device supports the choice of whether to broadcast battery voltage or battery percentage in the Sensor info
+frame, you can enter the ÔÇ£SETTING>>>Battery ADV modeÔÇØ and choose to broadcast one of them in this configuration
+item, this mode is mainly related to the byte23-byte24 in the Sensor info frame.
+
+                                            Figure 26: Battery ADV mode selection page
+If you select the broadcast mode as Battery Voltage, then byte23-byte24 inside the Sensor info broadcast frame will
+display the battery voltage, which is usually greater than 100. If you select the broadcast mode as Battery Percentage,
+then byte23-byte24 inside the Sensor info broadcast frame will display the battery.
+
+When you parse the broadcast, you can use the above logic to determine which mode it belongs to(whether the
+value is greater than 100), so you can decide whether you should display the battery voltage or the battery
+percentage.
+
+                                                                                      36
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+3.13 ADV Channel
+
+This configuration item allows you to configure the BLE Bluetooth advertising channels. By default, the product
+broadcasts on BLE channels 37(2402MHz), 38(2426MHz), and 39(2480MHz) simultaneously. You can configure the
+device to broadcast on a single channel. The advantage of broadcasting on a single channel is lower power
+consumption, but the downside is that it will increase the difficulty for scanning devices to detect the broadcast.
+
+                                               Figure 27: ADV channel selection page
+
+3.14 How to enable ÔÇ£Long rangeÔÇØ features
+
+In order to enhance the transmission range of the tag and expand the application scenarios as well as reduce the
+gateway deployment costs, we have optimized the firmware feature and integrated with coded PHY mode. Based on
+current firmware, ÔÇ£1M PHY ÔÇô LegacyÔÇØ and ÔÇ£Coded PHY ÔÇô Long RangeÔÇØ are supported already, and you can access to
+ÔÇ£SETTING >>>ADV modeÔÇØ and adopt the right one to your application scenarios.
+
+                                           Figure 28: How to enable ÔÇ£Long RangeÔÇØ mode
+
+                                                                                      37
+                                                                       Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                     MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2
+
+´âÿ Legacy --- 1M PHY
+´âÿ Long Rang --- Coded PHY
+
+Normally the transmission range in Coded PHY mode will be multiple times of transmission range in 1M PHY, and the
+advertisement payload format/content will be remaining with only the changes of advertisement type due to BLE 5.0
+new specifications.
+
+Here we have listed the ADV type difference between 1M PHY and Coded PHY. And for more introduction you can
+refer to link: https://www.bluetooth.com/bluetooth-resources/intro-to-bluetooth-low-energy-coded-phy/
+
+                                                    PHYs
+
+       Frame type                            LE 1M                               LE Coded
+
+           iBeacon       Connectable status  Non-connectable status  Connectable status Non-connectable status
+Eddystone ÔÇô URL/TLM/UID  ADV_IND + SCAN_RSP        ADV_SCAN_IND                  ADV_EXT_IND + AUX_ADV_IND
+                                                                                 ADV_EXT_IND + AUX_ADV_IND
+         Sensor info            ADV_IND         ADV_NONCONN_IND                  ADV_EXT_IND + AUX_ADV_IND
+                         ADV_IND + SCAN_RSP        ADV_SCAN_IND
+
+                         Table 10: ADV type difference between 1M and Coded PHY
+
+                                               38
+                         Copyright ┬® 2025 MOKO TECHNOLOGY LTD.
+                                                                                                             MK Sensor Series APP User Manual V1.2

+3.18 How to power off your device
+
+The app does not support a software shutdown function because the firmware is compatible with different product
+models of the company, some of which do not have button or hall switch. If the device without button/hall switch is
+shut down via software, it cannot be powered on again, therefore, for devices without buttons, shutdown is not
+supported or recommended. For devices with buttons/hall switch, we recommend using the button/hall switch for
+shutdown.
+The device's shutdown function via button/hall switch is disabled by default. You can refer to Section 3.7.5
+Disable/Enable your Beacon's button function to connect to the device, enable the button shutdown function, and
+then disconnect. After that, you can shut down the device using the button/hall switch.
+
+Additional Instructions´╝Ü
+The Tags with external button can power ON/OFF the device by button, such as S05T/L03/H4/H4P.
+The Tags without external button but with Hall switches can be powered ON/OFF by Hall magnet, such as
+L01/L01S/L02/L02S/L04/M2 Sensor/M6.
+The Tags without external button and without Hall switches do not support power OFF the device. such as
+M1P/M4/M4P/M4 Sensor
+´âÿ Mechanical button´╝Ü
+
+    ´ü¼ Power ON: Long press button for more than 3 seconds, and LED will keep red blinking for 3 seconds.
+    ´ü¼ Power OFF: Long press button for more than 3 seconds, and LED will keep solid red for 3 seconds.
+´âÿ Hall magnet´╝Ü
+    ´ü¼ Power ON: Hold a magnet close to the position marked with a Hall switch sign on the product and
+    keep it there for 3 seconds , and LED will keep red blinking for 3 seconds.
+    ´ü¼ Power OFF: Hold a magnet close to the position marked with a Hall switch sign on the product and
+    keep it there for 3 seconds and LED will keep solid red for 3 seconds.
+NOTICE: For more details about operating procedures for each product, please contact the sales of MOKO SMART
+to get related Product Specifications documents.
+

+
+4. FAQ
+
+General Questions
+´ü¼ What is RSSI?
+The RSSI (Received Signal Strength Indication) is a measurement of the power present in a received radio signal. It is
+usually displayed in dBm (Decibel-milliwatts). Low power devices, like Bluetooth Low Energy devices, are using very
+week power to transfer data, therefore the usual values of RSSI are between -100dBm (Very week signal) to around -
+20 dBm (strong signal).
+´ü¼ Why the RSSI values are different on two Android devices.
+By definition the RSSI value is a measurement of the power present in received radio signal. The signal strength
+depends on the antennas, device orientation, location, distance from the phone to target device, humidity etc.
+´ü¼ WhatÔÇÖs the difference between Advertising data and Scan Response data?
+In Bluetooth 4+ device, if not connected, continuously advertises with the Advertising data. When a scanner receives
+it, it may send the Scan request packet to obtain more advertising information. Then, the advertising device sends
+the Scan response data. Both packets may have up to 31 bytes.
+APP Questions
+´ü¼ Why the device name shows N/A?
+Device name is contained in scan response packet in ÔÇ£Sensor infoÔÇØ advertisement frame type, if ÔÇ£Sensor infoÔÇØ slot is
+not set, then APP will not achieve the device name and will shows N/A accordingly.
+
